金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 8 月 24 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美伊僵局延燒，川普喊經濟封鎖、油價再攻高：美伊 6 月備忘錄所訂 60 日期限於 8/17 屆滿
後破局，和談與油輪通行仍無進展。8/19 川普呼籲各國切斷伊朗經濟命脈，伊朗則強調「經
濟 D-Day」不會成功，卡達持續斡旋重啟荷莫茲通行。油價單週上漲逾 6%。若荷莫茲海峽
遲未恢復通行，油價恐再挑戰高點，須留意通膨及運價風險。
◼ 財政部擴大回購僅止血一日，長債與股市再承壓：美國 30 年期公債殖利率一度升破
5.33%，創 19 年新高；8/19 財政部宣布自 9/9 起，將 10 至 30 年期長債流動性回購上限由

20 億美元提高至 40 億美元。不過利多僅維持一日，沃爾瑪同店銷售亦放緩。整體而言，擴
大回購僅能減輕供給壓力，期限溢酬與財政赤字仍是影響長債走勢的關鍵。
◼ Fed 會議紀錄偏鷹，市場聚焦 Jackson Hole：7 月會議紀錄顯示，Fed 以 9 比 3 維持利率於
3.50%至 3.75%，部分官員傾向升息，多數官員認為若通膨未持續朝 2%目標下降，仍需進一
步緊縮政策。市場對 9 月政策路徑仍有分歧；8/27Jackson Hole 年會及新任主席 Warsh 於
8/28 的首場演說，將是重要觀察焦點。
市場展望
◼ 股市中長期偏多，正向看待後市：AI 基礎建設投資持續擴大，半導體需求中長期成長趨勢不
變。 不過 ， 本週將公布輝達財報 、PCE 數據及 Jackson Hole 會議訊息 ， 科技股短線可能震盪。
布局上建議關注營收與現金流穩健、訂單能見度高的半導體設備業者，並搭配電力設備、資安
及軍工等族群分散風險。展望後市，AI 投資持續帶動企業獲利成長，利率壓力趨緩，股市中長

期仍有表現空間，但短期評價修正與資金輪動風險須留意。
◼ 美國債務續創高，3~10 年期公債、投資級債相對有利：儘管貝森特表示將提出加強財政整合
計畫，短期有助長債價格，但美國公共債務已突破 40 兆美元。財政赤字與高利率環境下，再
融資及利息負擔難改善 ，20 年以上公債仍可能承受較高期限溢酬 。 此外 ，Fed 利率維持 3.50%
～3.75%，限制短端殖利率下行空間。相較之下，3～10 年期美債受財政供給與期限溢酬影響
較小；投資級債殖利率具吸引力，信用基本面穩健。整體而言，3～10 年期美債與投資級債風
險報酬較佳，超長天期債券則須留意財政供給、油價及期限溢酬上升所帶來的波動風險。

## Document 018: 2026年4～6月期のGDP（2次速報）結果

Report source: Unknown

1 / 2

2026 年 9 月 8 日

調査レポート

2026 年 4～6 月期の GDP（2 次速報）結果

～1 次速報から小幅上方修正されたが、景気の評価に変更なし～

調査部 主任研究員 藤田隼平

2026 年 9 月 8 日に内閣府が公表した 4～6 月期の実質 GDP（2 次速報値）は、前期比＋0.4%（年率換算   
＋1.4%）と、8 月に公表された 1 次速報値の同＋0.3%（同換算＋1.1%）から小幅ながら上方修正された。緩やか
な景気回復が続いているとの評価に変わりはなく、米国とイランの対立が逆風となったが、政府の物価高対策や
代替調達の進展などが下支えとなったこともあり、当初懸念されたような物価高や供給制約が景気全体を下押し
する事態は回避された。 
実質 GDP の需要項目別に 1 次速報からの 主な修正状況をみると、 まず個人消費が前期比－0.0% から   
＋0.0%に小幅に上方修正された。他方、 住宅投資は前期比－0.5%から同－0.6%に小幅に下方修正された。
企業部門では、財務省「法人企業統計」において季節調整済みの設備投資（ソフトウェアを含む・名目）が前期比
＋1.5%と増加したことから、GDP 統計の設備投資も前期比－1.2%から同－0.9%に上方修正された。民間在庫
は前回からほとんど変わらなかった。また、政府部門では、政府最終消費が前期比＋1.6%から同＋1.7%へ小幅
に上方修正された一方、公共投資は同－0.1%から同－0.5%へ下方修正された。なお、輸出、輸入ともに小幅に
下方修正されたが、外需寄与度に大きな修正はなかった。

（注）内需寄与度、民間在庫、外需寄与度は実質 GDP に対する寄与度（%ポイント）

### Page 2

 2 次速報値を踏まえた、2026 年 4～6 月期における需要項目別の動向（実質ベース）は以下の通り。

① 実質個人消費（前期比＋ 0.0%） 
 家計を取り巻く所得環境 は改善したものの （実質雇用者報酬は前期比＋ 1.0%）、イラン情勢の
悪化による消費者マインドの弱さもあり、 前期から横ばいとなった 。制度変更や価格上昇前の駆
け込みが一部に生じた影響もあって、 耐久財（前期比＋ 4.0% ）や半耐久財（同 ＋0.8% ）が増加
した一方、 非耐久財（ 同－1.9%）が加熱式たばこの値上げもあって落ち込んだほか、サービス
（同－0. 2%）も減少した。  
② 実質住宅投資（前期比－ 0.6%） 
 住宅投資に先行する住宅着工戸数は 2 四半期ぶりに増加したものの、床面積ベースでは伸び
悩んだこともあり 、住宅投資は 3 四半期ぶりに減少 した。 
③ 実質設備投資（前期比－ 0.9% ） 
 企業の投資意欲は良好なものの、建設 費をはじめ種々の 投資コスト が増加していることもあって 、
設備投資は 2 四半期連続で減少した。  
④ 実質民間在庫（前期比寄与度＋ 0.3%ポイント ） 
 2 月末からのイラン情勢悪化による供給制約懸念の高まりを受けて、メーカーが製品在庫 など
の積み増しを進めたことから、民間在庫はプラス 寄与となった。  
 なお、公的在庫は政府が石油備蓄の放出を進めたことから、前期比寄与度－ 0.5% ポイントとな
った。 
⑤ 実質政府最終消費（前期比＋1.7% ） 
 高校授業料無償化の拡充や小学校給食費の 無償化などもあり、 前期比で増加が続いた。  
⑥ 実質公共投資（前期比－ 0.5%） 
 名目では増加したものの、 工事コストの増加を受けた デフレーターの上昇に追いつ いておらず、
2 四半期ぶりに減少した 。 
⑦ 外需（前期比寄与度＋ 0.5%ポイント 、実質輸出：前期比＋ 0.4%、実質輸入：同－ 1.7%） 
 輸出は前期比＋ 0.4%と増加した。財はイラン情勢の悪化を受けて中東向けを中心に減少した
一方、サービスは研究開発サービスが一時的な要因により上振れたこともあって増加した。  
 輸入は前期比－ 1.7%と減少した。財は鉱物性燃料を中心に減少し、サービスもこのところ増加
が続いていた反動もあって減少した。  
 結果、輸出から輸入を差し引いた外需の寄与度は＋0.5% ポイントとプラスになった。

## Document 042: 2026年9月FOMCプレビュー ～利上げと据え置きそれぞれのメリット・デメリット～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月FOMCプレビュー ～利上げと据え置きそれぞれのメリット・デメリット～.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

US Trends

2026 年 9 月 8 日

1/6 
2026 年 9 月 FOMC プレビュー 
～利上げと据え置きそれぞれのメリット・デメリット～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  前田 和馬 
（℡：050-5473-3888）

（要旨） 
○ 9 月 FOMC（9/15～16 開催）に関して、9 月 7 日時点の FF 金利先物市場では約 6 割の確率で
0.25%pt の利上げ、4 割の確率で金利据え置きが織り込まれている。9 月 FOMC における政策
判断は、9/11 公表の8 月 CPI でインフレの減速傾向が保たれるかに大きく依存する。 
○ ウォーシュ議長は8 月28 日のジャクソンホール講演において、 「インフレが明確かつ十分な
速度で2%へ向かわなければ、 やるべき仕事がある」 と述べるなど、9 月FOMC における利上げ
の可能性を示唆した。一方、ウォラー理事やニューヨーク連銀・ウィリアムズ総裁は利上げ
に慎重な姿勢を示すなど、現時点において主要メンバー間の見解は統一されていない。 
○ 8 月 CPI が市場予想通りに着地し FRB が利上げを決定する場合、ウォーシュ議長は「インフ
レ抑制に向けた強い姿勢」や「金融政策の政治からの独立性」を示すことができよう。しか
し、11 月に中間選挙を控えるなか、トランプ大統領は利上げ判断を非難する可能性が高い。 
○ 一方、ウォーシュ発言後に利上げ観測が高まったにもかかわらず政策金利を据え置けば、金
融市場では FRB のコミュニケーション不信を巡る懸念が強まるだろう。とはいえ、こうした
言動不一致は FRB 高官発言の重要性を低下させ、ウォーシュ議長の望む「金融市場が自ら景
気動向を評価する（経済指標を注視する）」状態を形成するのに役立つ可能性もある。 
○ 今後の政策金利パスを見極めるうえでは、同時に公表されるドットチャートが注目される。
9 月に利上げに踏み切る場合には今後の利上げペース、金利が据え置かれる場合には 2026～
27 年における利上げの可能性が意識されるだろう。なお、ウォーシュ議長は 6 月 FOMC と同
様に自身の予測を提出しない可能性が高い。

### Page 2

2/6 
・予想が割れる 9 月FOMC 
9 月FOMC（9/15～16 開催）における政策金利判断は、金融市場の予想が大きく割れている。過
去 2 か月における9 月FOMCの政策予想をみると（資料 1）、7 月分の雇用統計（8/7 公表）や消費者
物価指数（8/12）が軟調だったことで据え置き予想が優勢だった一方、ウォーシュ議長のジャク
ソンホール会議講演（8/28）が予想に反してタカ派だったことで0.25%の利上げ予想が強まった。
その後、ウォラー理事など他のFRB高官がタカ派の見方に追随しなかったため、利上げ織り込みが
幾分剥落したものの、8 月雇用統計（9/4）が市場予想を上回ったことで再び利上げ予想が優勢と
なった。この結果、本稿執筆時点におけるFF金利先物市場（Fed Watch）において、約 6 割の確率
で 0.25%ptの利上げ、4 割の確率で金利据え置きが織り込まれている。なお、利上げとなれば2023
年 7 月以来、据え置きとなれば6 会合連続となる。

資料 1： 9 月 FOMC における利上げ織り込み 
  資料 2：市場が予想する各FOMC における政策金利
の確率分布（9/7 時点）

出所: CME、FRB、EIA より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所が作成

8 月地区連銀経済報告（9 月2 日公表：8 月 24 日までの情報に基づく）では前回報告の評価が概
ね据え置かれた。すなわち「12 地区中10 地区で経済活動は小幅から緩やかに成長した」とまとめ
られ、消費では高級品や観光、製造業では防衛やデータセンター関連の堅調さがそれぞれ指摘さ
れた。一方、インフレのペースは「前回報告から8 地区で横ばい、3 地区で減少、1 地区で増加」
と述べられた。また、雇用は「ごく僅かに増加」と前回表現の「総じて増加」から下方修正され
た。ただ、これは8 月雇用統計公表前の評価であることに留意が必要だ。8 月の非農業部門雇用者
数は前月差+16.2 万人と市場予想（+5.5 万人）を大幅に上回ったほか、過去2 か月分も上方修正
された。この結果、3 か月移動平均では+7.1 万人となるなど、高齢化やデータセンター需要を背
景に、医療・介護や卸・小売、建設業などで回復の動きが続いている。

### Page 3

3/6 
9 月FOMCまでには9/10 に 8 月生産者物価指数（PPI）、9/11 に8 月消費者物価指数（CPI）、9/16
に 8 月小売売上高が公表される。最大の注目は8 月CPIであり、市場予想は総合指数が前月比
+0.4%（7 月実績：+0.1%）とガソリン価格を中心に加速する一方、食料とエネルギーを除くコア指
数は+0.2%（同+0.2%）と横ばい圏の推移が見込まれている。コアCPIが市場予想を上回り8 月コア
PCE（9/30 公表）においてもインフレ加速が見込まれる場合、9 月FOMCにおける利上げ織り込みが
進むだろう。一方、過去2 か月と同様にインフレの減速傾向が確認されれば、据え置き予想がや
や強まる可能性がある。

・ウォーシュ議長のタカ派シフト 
7 月FOMCは 5 会合連続で政策金利が据え置かれ、声明文における景気・物価判断は前回から変更
がなかった。すなわち、経済活動は「中東紛争による不確実性の高まりにも関わらず、経済活動は
堅調なペースで拡大」 、 労働市場は 「雇用増は労働力人口の拡大と歩調を合わせており、 失業率はほ
ぼ変化していない」 、物価は「インフレ率は+2%目標と比べて高止まりしている。この背景には供給
ショックがエネルギーを含む一部のセクターの物価を押し上げていることがある」 と、 それぞれま
とめられた。本稿執筆時点において、こうした評価を大幅に変えうる材料は特段見当たらない。 
一方、ウォーシュ議長はジャクソンホール講演（8/28）において、8 月分の物価指標の結果が良
好でなければ9 月FOMCで利上げに踏み切る可能性を示唆した。 具体的には、自身の発言がフォワー
ドガイダンスではないと前置きしたうえで、 「今夏のPCEとCPIは予想よりも良好だった一方、基調
的なインフレが大きく改善したとは思えない」や「基調的なインフレが目標に向かって明確かつ十
分なスピードで進展しないならば、やるべきことがある」と述べた。ウォーシュ議長が利上げを提
案する場合、7 月FOMCでも利上げを主張した3 名の地区連銀総裁のほか（クリーブランド連銀のハ
マック総裁、 ミネアポリス連銀のカシュカリ総裁、 ダラス連銀のローガン総裁） 、 バー理事やクック
理事などの多くの執行部メンバーがこれに同調する可能性が高い。 
一方、利上げに慎重な向きも残る。9/2 にニューヨーク連銀のウィリアムズ総裁はインフレの緩
やかな鈍化傾向がみられるとし、現状の政策金利が適切な水準にあると指摘した。また、9/3 には
ウォラー理事が、一部品目の推計手法変更がコアPCEインフレ率の下方修正に繋がる可能性を指摘
したうえで、「インフレ率が目標に向けて進展していることを確認できれば、政策金利の据え置き
を支持する」と主張した。仮に 9 月FOMCにて利上げが決定される場合においても、ウォラー理事や
ボウマン理事は据え置きを主張し反対票を投じる可能性がある。

・それぞれの判断に対するメリデメ 
9 月FOMCの政策判断は今後公表される8 月分の物価指標に大きく依存する。一方、仮に物価動向
が概ね市場予想と一致する場合、9 月の政策判断は次のような意味合いを持つだろう。 
まず、利上げへと踏み切る場合、FRBは物価安定を重視する姿勢を改めて示すことができる。特に
足下でインフレ減速の兆しがみられるなか、基調的なインフレトレンドの低下が十分でないとして
利上げに踏み切ることは、FRBがインフレ抑制を重視する姿勢を鮮明にする。 また、 ウォーシュ議長

### Page 4

4/6 
は利下げを求めるトランプ政権への配慮から利上げに否定的との見方 が就任以前からあるものの、
9 月FOMCでの利上げはこうした見方を払しょくし、金融政策判断の政治的独立性を示すだろう。も
っとも、11 月の中間選挙を控えるなかでの利上げはトランプ政権の批判を招く可能性が高い。実
際、9/4 にトランプ大統領は 「米国の金利はどの国よりも低くあるべき」 、 バンス副大統領は 「イン
フレ指標は利下げを正当化する内容」とそれぞれ主張している。 
一方、政策金利を据え置く場合、 ウォーシュ議長のジャクソンホール講演を受けて利上げ観測が
高まっただけに、金融市場ではFRBのコミュニケーション手法に対する不信感を強めるだろう。ウ
ォーシュ議長は「インフレの明確かつ十分な進展」の基準を明言したわけではないが、一般論とし
て考えると、8 月分の物価指標がインフレ鈍化を明確に示す内容でなければ、据え置き判断との整
合性は取りにくい。また、金利据え置きは「ウォーシュ議長がトランプ政権に配慮し利上げに踏み
切れない」との見方を強めることになる。とはいえ、 据え置きの場合には金融市場がFRB高官発言へ
の注目度を低下させるとみられ、これはウォーシュ議長が望む「金融市場がFRB高官の発言を過度
に気にするのではなく、自ら景気・物価動向を判断する」状態に近づける効果を持つ。

・ドットチャートの示す政策パス 
 9 月FOMC後には四半期経済見通し （SEP）が公表される。 前回6 月時点の政策金利見通し （ドット
チャートの中央値）は、2026 年が 3.75%（0.5 回の利上げ） 、27 年は 3.625%（0.5 回の利下げ；現
在の金利水準）、28 年は3.375%（1 回の利下げ）となり、前回 3 月FOMC（2026～27 年にそれぞれ1
回ずつの利下げを予想）から上方修正された。仮に9 月FOMCで利上げへと踏み切る場合、今後の焦
点は追加的な利上げの有無やその回数に移行する。例えば、7 月FOMCで利上げを主張したダラス連
銀のローガン総裁は「短期的に控えめな利上げ」に言及する一方、ミネアポリス連銀のカシュカリ
総裁は複数回の利上げの可能性を指摘している。 このため、新たなドットチャートでは、26～27 年
における追加の利上げ回数、28 年に利下げサイクルへ戻るのか否か、さらに旺盛なAI投資を背景に
中立金利（longer run）の上昇が示唆されるのか、などが注目される。一方、9 月FOMCが据え置き
判断となる場合、26～27 年において利上げを予想するタカ派と据え置きを見込むハト派の分布が
焦点となるだろう。

5/6

いと指摘した。 一方、9月で利上げを支持するかを巡っては 「もう少
し情報が必要」と述べるなど、データを注視する姿勢を示した

「今夏のPCEとCPIは予想よりも良好だった一方、基調的なインフレ

ション手法を巡っては「平時において、フォワードガイダンスの役
割は限定的かつ制限されるべき」と従来の主張を繰り返した 
9/1 バー理事* 
「インフレは5年以上にわたり高止まりしている」と述べ、9月FOMC
において「インフレ率が十分に鈍化しているようにみえない場合、
断固として利上げを行うべき」と明言した

ニューヨーク連銀

準」と述べるなど、9月FOMCで様子見姿勢をとる可能性を示唆した。

いるとの見方を示した

9月FOMCにおける政策判断は 8月のインフレ動向に強く依存すると
し、「2%目標への進展が継続するならば、現状維持を支持する」と
述べた。 また、FRBの政策反応関数をどのようにコミュニケーション
するかを巡って、野球の球審のように完璧なストライクゾーンでは
なく「大まかな目安」を示す必要性を主張した

美联储还是不如市场乐观

2026 年 9 月美联储议息会议点评   
当前美国经济动能中包括了财政扩张的影响，长期美债扭转颓势 ，仍需美国
财政赤字暂时改善及地缘冲突缓解。 
 美联储 9 月加息仍可以定义为“预防性加息”，对美国经济的乐观程度
不及市场。美联储 9 月利率预测点阵图显示，提交预测的最激进委员也
仅预测本轮加息 3 个 25BP(含本次)，预测中值为本轮加息 2 次。而 9 月
会议前，期货市场对本轮加息的隐含预测接近 3.8 次 25BP 加息。当然，
美联储和美国市场的预期也不一定就是对的。 
 相对 6 月会议，美联储对美国经济潜力更加乐观，但乐观程度不及市场。
沃什表示，鉴于美国经济韧性以及更大潜力的可能，他在 FOMC 会议中
感受到了乐观态度。这种乐观的直接表现就是点阵图对长期利率水平的
预测中值提高到了 3.25%，而 6 月的预测中值为 3%。不过，点阵图展现
的加息路径仍具有“预防性特征”：在加息以前，联邦基金利率仍高于
本次点阵图预测的长期利率，预期中的两次加息，或主要是为防止通胀
“脱锚”；待通胀回落后，美联储仍将重启降息 。如沃什所说，“加息
将确保相对价格变化不会扩大，不会对经济产生二级和三级影响”。  
 然而市场可能对长期利率水平有更高期望， 在加息预期达到 3.8 次的情况
下，美股科技股也没有出现非常明显的动荡。因此，在美联储预期不如
市场乐观，或者说加息不如市场预期“鹰派”的情况下， 我们预计本次
加息不会很快显现对科技股的很大利空。 
 我们在前期报告中提到，当前美联储加息对美债长期收益率未必是持久
利空的，相反，通过缓解 市场对美国货币政策长期不稳定性的担忧，还
有可能转为利好(《加息可能降收益率吗？》20260913)。 
 不过，从“格林斯潘之谜”的经验来看，加息过程中 的美债长期收益率
下行可能要伴随着市场对经济基本 面或者权益市场前景的预期改变 。在
“格林斯潘之谜”现象出现的同时，美股市场 就有一波明显回调，回调
阶段也与美债长期收益率不升反降的主要阶段大致重合。如果当前市场
对美国经济前景， 特别是AI 相关产业投资前景的乐观情绪没有根本改变，
美债彻底扭转颓势的难度仍然较高。 
 当前美国经济动能中包 含了财政扩张的影响，包括由于关税退税造成的
被动财政扩张，对美国财政政策不稳定性的担忧更是美债长期性利空的
重要根源（《美债的“近忧与远虑”》20260822）。 
 综合来看，我们认为美联储 9 月加息有助于重塑市场关于其对抗通胀的
信心，缓解对美国货币政策长期不稳定性的担忧，可能有助于缓解美债
长期收益率的上行 压力。但长期美债要扭转颓势，仍需美国财政赤字有
暂时改善及地缘冲突缓解。 
 对我国市场而言，我们预计本次加息通过科技股映射对我国股市的影响
也比较有限。不过，以美国为代表的发达经济体收紧货币政策，预计对
我国商品出口的增长有一定影响。而由于国内 零售消费和固定资产投资
增长放缓，出口对我国经济动能的相对重要性有所提高。因此，美联储
本次加息，不但很难对我国债市产生利空影响，反而可能略有利好作用。 
 风险提示： 国内外实体经济出现超预期变化；国内政策超预期收紧；国
际地缘政治紧张局势升级等。

公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

上銀 26Q2 受惠於產品漲價效益顯現及 UTR 提升，單季毛利率攀
升至 34.17%。單季合併營收達 75.08 億元，QoQ 17.70% 、YoY 
26.71%。營業利益與稅後淨利分別大幅成長至 10.34 億元與 8.48
億元，帶動單季 EPS 攀升至 2.40 元。 
展望後市， 研究部對上銀營收表現維持正向看待 。 目前終端需求持
續暢旺，產品交期維持高位，線性滑軌能見度約 4 至 5 個月，滾
珠螺桿最長可達 6 個月 。 儘管地緣政治因素推升鋼材 、 包材與潤滑
油等原物料價格 ， 但公司具備彈性定價能力且維持多元採購策略，
漲價效益足以抵銷成本上揚。伴隨26Q3 完整反映漲價產品銷售，
預估單季營收達 80.92 億元，QoQ 7.77%，YoY 34.97%，EPS 上
看 2.81 元。放眼 2026 至 2027 年，亞洲半導體需求穩固、歐洲軍
工與航太產業接棒成為新動能 ， 整體訂單需求無虞 。 預估上銀2026
年與 2027 年營收將分別達 296.90 億元與 329.36 億元 ， 維持雙位
數年增幅，YoY 分別為 22.37%與 10.93%。在營運規模提升帶動
下，預估 2026 年、2027 年 EPS 將分別達 9.28 元與 11.87 元。 
評價方面，上銀過去五年 PER 主要集中於 20 倍至 40 倍區間。考
量公司營收與獲利將呈現雙位數成長上升趨勢，產業結構持續上
行，研究部以2027 年預估 EPS 11.87 元作為評價基礎，並給予歷
史區間中緣偏上之 35x 本益比，目標價為 420 元，給予「買進」
之投資建議。  
⚫   ⚫ 公司基本資料 
上銀科技(2049)成立於 1989 年，主要從事線性滑軌(GW)、滾
螺桿(BS)、工業機器人、機器手臂、迴轉工作台、減速機、精
密軸承及醫療設備之研究開發、設計、製造、銷售、維修及售
前售後服務，同時為全球第三大滾珠螺桿廠和線性滑軌廠，僅
次於日本 THK、NSK。公司以自有品牌 「HIWIN」 行銷全球，
海外子公司橫跨德國、美國、日本、瑞士、捷克、法國，

漲價效益逐漸顯現，加上亞洲市場需求延續帶動交期延長；其
中 ， 機器人產品受惠於半導體需求強勁 ， 出貨量年增達 7 成。
第二季合併營收達 75.08 億元 ，QoQ 17.70%，YoY 26.71%。

⚫ 預估營收獲利季增，帶動 26Q3 單季 EPS 達 2.81 元 
上銀 8 月營收 27.08 億元 ，MoM 0.98%，YoY 42.30%；1-8
月累計營收 192.77 億元，YoY 23.03% 。研究部預期，9 月
營收有望延續雙位數年增態勢，預估  26Q3 營收將達 80.92 
億元，QoQ 7.77%，YoY 34.97%。 
獲利表現方面，考量第三季將完整反映產品漲價效益，且營收
規 模 擴 張 帶 動  UTR 提 升 ， 預 估  26Q3 毛 利 率 將 達 
35.43%，季增 1.27 個百分點，年增 6.83 個百分點。預估
單季營業利益達 12.00 億元 ，QoQ 16.11%，YoY 192.40%；
稅後淨利 9.94 億元，QoQ 17.25% ，YoY 122.90% ，單季 
EPS 預估為 2.81 元。

⚫ 全球需求延續，獲利有望持續成長，估 2026 年 EPS 9.28 元 
研究部對上銀後市營收維持正向看待，係因以下：(1) 終端需
求持續暢旺，產品交期維持高位； (2) 公司具備彈性定價能
力，漲價效益足以覆蓋成本上揚；(3) 半導體產業需求穩固，
持續帶動滾珠螺桿與機器人等相關產品出貨。 
訂單能見度方面，目前整體產品交期維持高檔，線性滑軌約  
4-5 個月 ， 滾珠螺桿最長可達 6 個月 。 觀察各區域下游需求：
亞洲市場維持暢旺，其中中國受惠於電動車、電子與半導體產
業持續擴張，帶動製造設備與傳動元件需求；東南亞受惠全球
供應鏈重組，除傳統傳動元件外， 亦間接推升機器人產品需
求；台灣則持續受惠半導體與科技業資本支出，帶動螺桿與機
器人穩健成長。歐洲市場方面，儘管德國汽車工業仍顯疲態，
但軍工與航太產業已成為新成長動能，加上捷克、波蘭等中歐
製造核心需求保持穩定，有效支撐歐洲營運。

### Page 1

Taiwan Industrials

What’s new: Eternal Precision Mechanics (EPM) reported its August 2026 
revenue after market hours on 9 September 2026.

What’s the impact: August revenue beat expectations. EPM reported 
August 2026 revenue of TWD359.8m, up 95.7% MoM and 111.5% YoY. 
QTD revenue reached 88.4% of our previous 3Q26 forecast and 74.2% of 
the Bloomberg consensus. Given the faster-than-expected revenue 
recognition schedule and a sharp increase in recognised high-end 
equipment units, which should drive a more favourable product mix, we 
raise our 3Q26 revenue forecast by 33% to TWD972.7m, gross margin by 
3.1ppts to 48.5%, and EPS by 92% to TWD1.86.

Vacuum laminators are emerging as the next ABF bottleneck. Despite 
a partial conversion of backlog into revenue in August, EPM’s backlog 
continued to increase from TWD810m at end-July to TWD880m in early 
September. With lead times extending further to 36 months, vacuum 
laminators are emerging as the next bottleneck in the ABF capacity 
expansion cycle. Given the robust demand from ABF substrate makers, we 
see upside potential to EPM’s capacity expansion plans. On margins, we 
expect continued product-mix improvement and the full realisation of price 
increases to lift gross margin by 6.6ppts/1.3ppts YoY to 53.2%/54.5% in 
2027/28E, driving a 2026-28E EPS CAGR of 141%.

Moulding film adoption opens a new growth avenue. Traditional 
transfer and compression moulding typically use granular, powder, or liquid-
type compounds for encapsulation. During our visit to SEMICON, Japanese 
material suppliers Nagase (8012 JP, JPY1,150.5, Outperform [2]; covered 
by Masayuki Nagano) and Resonac (4004 JP, JPY16,070, Outperform [2]; 
covered by Hidemitsu Umebayashi) noted growing customer interest in 
moulding films. These offer better filling performance, better warpage 
control and higher flowability. This makes them increasingly suitable for 
advanced packaging applications such as panel level packaging and 
component-embedded substrates. EPM supplies moulding-film laminators 
for both applications, with revenue contribution expected to begin in 2027.

What we recommend: We lift our 2026-28E EPS by 22-26% to reflect the 
full realisation of price increases and improving product mix. We reaffirm 
our Buy (1) rating and raise our 12-month TP to TWD720 (from TWD600) 
based on an unchanged target PER of 40x, applied to our 1-year forward 
EPS forecast. Key downside risk: slower-than-expected substrate capex 
growth.

How we differ: We likely conduct a more comprehensive analysis than 
others on ABF industry demand and moulding film development.

9 September 2026

Vacuum laminators: the next ABF bottleneck

Company profile 
Eternal was established in 2022 through the spin-off of Eternal Material’s equipment division and its 
subsequent merger with Nikko-Materials, a wholly owned Japanese subsidiary. The company was listed 
in January 2026 and is the global leader in IC substrate vacuum laminators, serving all major substrate 
manufacturers worldwide.

ESG analysis

Update Date: 26 Jun 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Eternal demonstrates a solid corporate governance structure supported by a balanced and
diversified board composition. The company’sboard consists of 7 members, including 3
independent directors, with backgrounds spanning industry operations, finance, accounting,
legal, and academia, enabling comprehensive oversight across business and governance
functions.
Capital management 2
Eternal adopts a prudent capital management strategy, supported by a stable shareholder
structure and disciplined financial policy. The company is majority-owned by Eternal Materials
(over 60% stake), providing long-term strategic backing and financial stability.
Related party &
transaction 1
Eternal discloses related-party transactions transparently and enforces strict governance
procedures to manage potential conflicts of interest. Board members are required to recuse
themselves from discussions and voting on matters involving personal or affiliated interests,
such as asset acquisitions, patent licensing, and remuneration decisions.
S Product quality &
safety 1
Eternal maintains strict product quality control, covering design, manufacturing, and delivery
processes. The company emphasizes equipment reliability and performance stability, supported
by long-term client relationships and low defect risks, ensuring high customer satisfaction and
consistent product safety standards.
E Product design &
lifecycle management 2
Eternal focuses on enhancing product durability and energy efficiency through continuous R&D
innovation. Its equipment design emphasizes precision, extended lifecycle and reduced material
waste, aligning product development with resource efficiency and long-term environmental
sustainability goals.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

## Document 083: 260909_daiwa_testing-interface

Source PDF: all report pdf/260909_daiwa_testing-interface.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: Driven by strong AI/HPC demand, all three companies set new revenue 
milestones: Winway (TWD 1.71bn, +6.6% MoM, +233.4% YoY), MPI (TWD 2.05bn, 
+13.4% MoM, +69.9% YoY), and CHPT (TWD 638m, +3.9% MoM, +54.3% YoY). With 
Winway’s capacity expansions tracking ahead of schedule, MPI set to begin shipping CPO 
insertion 3 products in 4Q26 alongside ASP hikes in late-3Q26, and CHPT nearing a 
qualification for its turnkey CPU ASIC project, the sector offers resilient sequential growth 
visibility through 2H26 and potential earnings upside heading into 2027.

What’s new: Following the conclusion of SEMICON Taiwan, we have observed a marked 
uptick in investor enquiries regarding the semiconductor test interface supply chain. As 
AI/HPC architectural transitions, and the increasing complexity of 2.5D/3D packaging drive 
structural content gains, investors have focused increasingly on capacity visibility and 
technological roadmaps across the socket and probe card segments. In the note, we recap 
the latest August monthly revenue figures and synthesize key market dynamics across our 
coverage universe.

WinWay Technology (6515 TT, TWD7,280, Buy [1]) 
• Aug revenue result: Monthly revenue reached TWD 1.71bn ( +6.6% MoM, +233.4% 
YoY), bringing 1-8M26 cumulative revenue to TWD 9.81bn (+97.9% YoY). Jul-Aug sales 
reached 72% of our 3Q26E revenue, tracking ahead of our expectations.

• Product mix & catalysts: Growth remains anchored by aggressive tape-outs from 
North American GPU leaders and cloud hyperscalers. Shipments of proprietary coaxial 
test sockets and advanced wafer-level vertical probe cards expanded sharply. While the 
market may expect meaningful revenue contributions from CPO sockets or probe cards, 
we believe it will account for mid-single digit in 2027E and high-single digit in 2028E.

• Outlook: The company maintained a constructive stance on order visibility and 
persistent demand-supply tightness. Management reaffirmed that 2Q26 marked the 
near-term cyclical trough, guiding for sequential revenue expansion on a QoQ basis 
moving forward. Our supply chain checks indicate that capacity expansion execution is 
tracking ahead of schedule compared with last month. With Winway poised to bring 
additional capacity online in 2027, we suggest investors monitor the company’s 2H26 
earnings call (mid-Nov) for further clarity on its 2027 capex and capacity roadmap.

• Valuation: We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD10,000, based on a 
target PER of 70x applied to our 4-quarter-forward EPS forecast. Based on our 
2027/28E EPS, the stock is currently trading at PERs of 36.9x/19.5x, vs. its previous 
highs of 80-90x. Downside risks: heavy reliance on major US AI chipmakers and; 2) 
intensifying competition that could erode design wins.

MPI Corporation (6223 TT, TWD5,615, Buy[1]) 
• Aug revenue result: Monthly revenue surpassed the TWD 2bn milestone for the first 
time, reaching TWD 2.05bn (+13.4% MoM, +69.9% YoY), bringing 1-8M26 cumulative 
revenue above TWD 13.0bn. Jul-Aug sales reached 63% of our 3Q26E revenue 
estimate, tracking slightly below our expectations.

• Product mix & catalysts: Vertical Probe Cards (VPC) and MEMS probe cards are 
operating at near-full capacity to support volume ramps in HPC, AI accelerators, and 
high-speed networking ICs. MPI’s AST (Advanced Semiconductor Test) and Thermal 
test chambers also maintained solid delivery momentum to European and US 
automotive and R&D laboratories. CPO insertion 3 engineering test equipment is 
scheduled for delivery in 4Q26, setting the stage for mass production orders in 1H27.

• Outlook: Although management’s muted 3Q26 guidance initially fuelled investor 
scepticism, and the cumulative 2M26 run-rate is tracking slightly behind our quarterly 
forecast, we view the market’s pessimism as overly conservative. Strong late-quarter 
shipment seasonality, coupled with concentrated September revenue recognition for 
high-end VPC/MEMS probe cards under the newly effective pricing schedule should 
help bridge the gap and keep 3Q26 on track to meet or exceed our top-line 
expectations. Looking into 4Q26 and early 2027, the commercial rollout of CPO 
insertion 3 equipment and continued certification progress for insertion 2 provide a multi-
year growth runaway and durable operating leverage.

• Valuation: We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD8,415, based on a 
target PER of 65x applied to our 4-quarter-forward EPS forecast. Based on our 
2027/28E EPS, the stock is currently trading at PERs of 42.9x/26.0x, vs. its peak of 80x. 
Downside risks: 1) Competitive and pricing pressure; and 2) delays due to AI/HPC 
slowdown.

Chunghwa Precision Test Tech (CHPT, 6510 TT, TWD3,610, Buy [1]) 
• Aug revenue result: August revenue totalled TWD 638m (+3.9% MoM, +54.3% YoY), 
bringing 1-8M26 cumulative revenue to TWD 4.25bn (+33.14% YoY). Jul-Aug sales 
reached 66% of our 3Q26E revenue, tracking in line with our expectations. In response 
to Taiwan Stock Exchange (TWSE) inquiries following recent share price volatility, CHPT 
disclosed its August financial results. Single-month EPS came in at TWD 5.29 (net 
income of TWD 174m, +95% YoY), implying a quarterly EPS run-rate of TWD 15.87 (vs 
Daiwa estimate of TWD 16.08 and market consensus of TWD 17.52)

• Product mix & catalysts: Top-line momentum was broad-based across both the AI 
GPU and ASIC segments. Management highlighted ongoing footprint expansion and 
continued efforts to identify additional sites to scale manufacturing capacity. Moving 
forward, steady deliveries of AI GPU test boards, alongside the forthcoming volume 
ramp of custom AI ASICs, should underpin sequential revenue growth.

• Outlook: Our supply chain checks indicate CHPT is competing for three AI/HPC probe 
card turnkey programs, comprising a CPU ASIC, an AI ASIC program for a US 
automotive client, and another AI ASIC program for Taiwanese clients. The automaker 
qualification process is progressing smoothly, and CHPT plans to ship engineering 
probe cards for validation in November, with validation results expected by end-2026. 
Given the superior content value of probe card solutions versus board-only products, a 
successful qualification would provide incremental top-line upside into late-2026, while 
serving as a significant catalyst for 2027 revenue upgrades and valuation expansion.

• Valuation: We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD4,000, based on a 
target PER of 45x applied to our 4-quarter-forward EPS forecast. Based on our 
2027/28E EPS, the stock is currently trading at PERs of 30.2x/24.0x, vs. its peak of 68x. 
Downside risks: 1) project delays and utilisation risk; and 2) loss of market share.

## Document 088: 260910_gs_TSMC

Source PDF: all report pdf/260910_gs_TSMC.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Presenters: Wendell Huang, SVP & CFO  
Bottom Line: We have three key takeaways: (1) TSMC continues to view AI as a 
multi-year megatrend that remains at a very early stage, with lower compute costs 
expected to accelerate AI adoption and ultimately drive greater demand for 
advanced silicon; (2) execution in Arizona continues to progress well, with Phase 1 
achieving yields comparable to Taiwan and already generating profits, while TSMC 
expects approximately 30% of its N2-and-beyond capacity to be located outside 
Taiwan in five years, primarily in Arizona; and (3) despite dilution from the N2 ramp 
and overseas expansion, management remains comfortable achieving its long-term 
gross margin target of 56% and above, supported by disciplined capacity planning, 
high utilization, productivity improvements, cost reductions, and pricing that reflects 
TSMC’s value. 
Key Takeaways

AI remains in the early stages of a multi-year megatrend: TSMC continues to n
see increasing adoption of AI. Management also highlighted the shift from 
generative AI toward agentic AI, which should broaden compute demand 
beyond accelerators to CPUs. TSMC remains confident in the durability of the AI 
cycle based on discussions not only with its direct customers but also with its 
customers’ customers. Recent visits of US CSPs further reinforced management’s 
confidence, as cloud service providers appeared well prepared in areas including 
power and broader infrastructure deployment. 
Lower compute costs should drive greater consumption and silicon demand: n
Management believes open-source models and continued improvements in 
computing efficiency will reduce token costs, leading to greater AI 
consumption and broader adoption, supporting incremental demand for 
advanced silicon. TSMC has already incorporated this dynamic into its capacity 
planning and continues to expand aggressively to support what it views as

### Page 2

structural, multi-year demand growth.
Disciplined demand validation remains critical amid aggressive expansion: n
TSMC uses a combination of top-down and bottom-up analysis to validate demand.
Its top-down assessment considers macroeconomic conditions, semiconductor
demand, fabless/system companies, the foundry industry, etc, while its bottom-up
approach evaluates demand across different segments and incorporates feedback
from customers and customers’ customers. Management acknowledged that

semiconductors will remain cyclical, but believes correctly identifying the underlying
megatrend is more important than attempting to forecast every short-term
fluctuation.
Arizona execution is demonstrating that TSMC’s manufacturing model can n
travel: TSMC reiterated that overseas expansion is based on customer demand for
geographic flexibility and an appropriate level of government support. Arizona
Phase 1, using N4 technology, is in operation, with yields comparable to Taiwan,

satisfactory quality and reliability, and positive profitability. Management views
this as an important milestone because it demonstrates internally and to customers
that TSMC can produce wafers in the US with the same quality as in Taiwan.
Equipment move-in for Phase 2, which will use N3 technology, is expected to begin
soon; construction of Phase 3 has started; and preparation work for Phase 4 and the
first US advanced-packaging facility is underway.

Arizona has substantial room for further expansion: In addition to its original n
Arizona development, TSMC has acquired another parcel of land capable of
accommodating an additional five to six fabs, supporting its recently
announced incremental US$100bn investment. Based on its current plan, TSMC
expects approximately 30% of its N2-and-beyond capacity to be located outside
Taiwan in five years, primarily in Arizona. However, the most advanced technologies

will continue to ramp first in Taiwan because the early-stage technology transfer
requires close collaboration and frequent movement of engineers among R&D and
manufacturing sites. Overseas deployment will generally follow after the technology
has stabilized in Taiwan.
Customers increasingly value and are willing to pay for geographic flexibility: n
Management noted that customers initially requested only a modest amount of
geographically diversified supply, but their requirements have increased as TSMC has

demonstrated successful execution in Arizona. TSMC believes this geographic
flexibility represents incremental value and intends to reflect that value in its pricing,
with customers increasingly willing to recognize the associated premium.
Technology, manufacturing excellence, capacity, and trust underpin TSMC’s n
competitive moat: Management believes customers select a foundry partner based
on access to the best technology, the ability to ramp rapidly into high-volume

manufacturing with attractive economics, sufficient capacity, and confidence that
the foundry will not compete with its customers. TSMC is confident in maintaining
this lead by continuing to advance its roadmap. Management also highlighted its
record of rapidly ramping new technologies, the scale of its wafer and
advanced-packaging investments, and its consistent delivery against customer
10 September 2026   2
Goldman Sachs TSMC (2330.TW)For the exclusive use of KEVINLU@LENOVO.COM

e92c7a75ab8b4efbba794e6b187208c8

### Page 3

commitments as key elements of customer trust that cannot be replicated through
CapEx or engineering recruitment alone.
Pricing remains strategic rather than opportunistic: TSMC does not intend to raise n
prices simply because near-term utilization is high, nor reduce prices simply because
utilization temporarily weakens. Instead, the company’s approach is to price
sustainably and consistently based on the value it provides, while ensuring that
customers can remain successful over the long term. Management noted that

TSMC’s structural profitability has continued to improve, with its long-term gross
margin target increasing from 50% five years ago to 53% and now 56% and above.
Leading-edge wafer and advanced-packaging capacity remain tight: n
Management continues to build capacity wherever possible to meet customer
demand. N3 demand remains particularly strong: unlike prior technology cycles,
when demand for a node typically began declining two to three years after its

introduction, TSMC is adding further N3 capacity and still does not expect supply
to be sufficient. The company is also expanding advanced-packaging capacity to
address strong accelerator demand. Outside TSMC, management identified power
and memory as potential bottlenecks cited by other parts of the AI supply chain,
while emphasizing that foundry capacity itself remains difficult to expand because
fabs require two to three years to build and another one to two years to ramp.

TSMC’s disclosed AI revenue understates its broader exposure: Management n
reiterated that its disclosed AI revenue definition is relatively narrow and primarily
captures GPUs, custom AI accelerators and memory base die. It excludes CPUs and
networking products because TSMC cannot reliably determine whether each chip is
ultimately deployed in an AI system. As agentic AI drives incremental demand across
x86, Arm, and RISC-V CPUs, as well as networking and other supporting silicon,

TSMC’s total AI-related exposure is therefore broader than its reported
AI-accelerator revenue. The company intends to retain its existing definition until it
can more accurately distinguish the end use of these products.
No visible barrier to continued transistor scaling: TSMC is currently ramping N2, n
with A14 expected approximately two years later, while development of the
subsequent node is already underway. Management does not currently see a

technical issue preventing delivery of the next node or continued advancement
beyond the existing roadmap. TSMC views close collaboration with customers as a
key foundry advantage, allowing the company to develop each technology against
specific customer performance, power, density, and economic requirements.
Cross-node capacity optimization is an underappreciated advantage: TSMC n
highlighted its ability to generate additional output through productivity

improvements and to convert tools and capacity across technology nodes. For
example, the company can use portions of its N5 capacity to support N3 demand
and can also optimize capacity across less-adjacent nodes where technically
feasible. Management views this cross-node flexibility as a distinctive element of
TSMC’s manufacturing excellence, allowing the company to respond more
quickly to demand changes while reducing the risk of capacity obsolescence.

Advanced packaging is strategic primarily because it enables wafer growth: n
10 September 2026   3
Goldman Sachs TSMC (2330.TW)For the exclusive use of KEVINLU@LENOVO.COM
e92c7a75ab8b4efbba794e6b187208c8

### Page 4

CoWoS capacity remains tight, and TSMC already started outsourcing selected 
packaging processes to OSAT partners to support additional growth. Management 
emphasized that advanced packaging is intended primarily to ensure and enable 
wafer demand rather than serve as a standalone profit pool. TSMC is therefore 
developing multiple packaging architectures, including panel-level packaging, etc, 
while remaining open to customers using alternative packaging providers, as either 
approach ultimately supports demand for TSMC wafers. 
Advanced-packaging execution remains a key differentiator: TSMC noted that its n
current advanced-packaging capability supports approximately 5.5x reticle size, 
with a roadmap to reach approximately 14x reticle sizes within the next several 
years. While competing roadmaps may also appear attractive, management believes 
execution and the ability to deliver in high-volume manufacturing will ultimately 
determine competitive outcomes. TSMC does not believe competitors can use 
advanced-packaging wins to meaningfully displace its leading-edge wafer 
business, as foundry and packaging remain fundamentally different businesses. 
A unified TSMC culture supports overseas manufacturing execution: TSMC’s n
objective is not to create separate Taiwan and US cultures, but to establish a 
consistent TSMC culture centered on integrity, commitment, innovation, and 
customer trust (ICIC). Locally hired Arizona employees spend six to eighteen months 
training and working in Taiwan, while experienced Taiwan-based employees are 
assigned to Arizona to work alongside local teams. Over time, the number of Taiwan 
assignees should decline as locally trained employees assume greater responsibility. 
TSMC is applying this approach across its overseas manufacturing sites. 
Management remains comfortable with its 56%-and-above long-term gross n
margin target: TSMC reiterated its 6 major drivers of profitability: new-technology 
ramp, pricing, cost, product mix, utilization, and FX. The N2 ramp is expected to 
dilute 2026 GM by approximately 2-3ppts, but its profitability should improve over 
time toward the corporate average; management also noted that N2’s structural 
profitability is better than N3 at a comparable stage. Overseas expansion is 
expected to dilute GM by 2-3ppts during the early portion of the current five-year 
period, then widening to 3-ppts later as more overseas fabs begin production. Each 
new fab will initially create dilution before improving along its learning curve. Taking 
these puts and takes together, management remains very comfortable that 
disciplined capacity planning, healthy utilization, cost reduction, productivity 
gains, and pricing that reflects TSMC’s value will enable the company to deliver 
for shareholders and achieve its long-term gross margin target ‘of 56% and 
higher’ with focus on the ‘higher’ side. 
Valuation/Risks

TSMC (Buy): Our 12-month price target is NT$3,100, which is derived by applying a 
target P/E multiple of 22x to our 2027E EPS. Our 22x target P/E is benchmarked against 
1.0stdv above its 5-year trading average. For the ADR (TSM), we have a 12-month price 
target of US$620, based on a USD/TWD rate of 30.0 and an ADR premium of 20%. Key

downside risks to our views: (1) further deterioration in end-demand recovery 
impacting capacity utilization; (2) slower customer node migrations; (3) slowdown in AI 
investment resulting in lower long-term semiconductor content growth; (4) poor 
yields/execution resulting in worse-than-expected profitability; (5) stronger 
competition resulting in ASP/profitability erosion; and (6) unfavorable FX trend or 
higher-than-expected cost increase weighing on the margin outlook.

### Page 3

Earnings revision: We factor in SZS Aug revenues, revise down 2026E earnings by 11%, 
revise up 2027E earnings by 1%, and keep 2028E earnings largely unchanged. We revise 
down 2026E revenues mainly on lower-than-expected Aug revenues, considering longer 
time for new projects to reflect on company’s revenues/ earnings. We revise up 2027 
earnings mainly on higher revenues, reflecting our positive view on SZS as the key 
beneficiary of rising foldable phones.

Valuation: We are Buy rated on SZS with a 12-month target price of NT$300 (vs. 
previously at NT$286) on lower earnings. Our TP is based on a 21.9x 2027E P/E (vs. 
previously at 21.0x), which is derived from the updated sector correlation of 2027E P/E 
vs. 2027E-28E EPS YoY (Exhibit 8), with re-rating supported by positive view on rising 
foldable phone penetration. The 21.9x 2027E P/E is also within SZS’s trading range since 
Aug 2019.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - SZS 
Valuation: We use a near-term P/E vs. EPS growth correlation to derive our target price 
for SZS, consistent with our Taiwan Technology coverage. Our target 2027E P/E multiple 
of 21.9x for SZS is based on the sector correlation of 2027E P/E vs. 2027-28E earnings 
growth. We thus derive our 12-month target price at NT$300. We are Buy rated. Key 
risks: Weaker-than-expected headset hinges demand; slower-than-expected foldable 
phone penetration; potential competition from more suppliers; soft market demand.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

壹、投 資 建 議

一、 2Q26 營收 13.76 億元， EPS 1.79 元 
2Q26 合併營收 13.76 億元 ，QoQ +35.3% 、YoY +23.9% ，隨 AI Server 
Power 需求加速拉貨，單季營運明顯轉強。毛利率 28.8% ，QoQ 
+0.4ppts 、 YoY +5.2ppts ；營業利益率 22.3% ，較去年同期提升
5.7ppts ，帶動營業利益 3.06 億元、 YoY +66.3% ，獲利增幅顯著高
於營收 。 稅後淨利3.06 億元 、YoY +671% ，EPS 1.79 元；累 計1H26
營收 23.92 億元、 YoY +16% ，EPS 2.98 元。獲利結構改善主要來
自產品組合由去年同期車用轉向 AI 大尺寸、高功率及高壓
MLCC ，AI 營收占比由 1Q26 的 6.5% 提升至 2Q26 的 9.2% ，帶動
毛利率與營益率同步走升。 8 月單月營收達  5.52 億元，月增
2.69% ，年增 54.27% ，寫下自 2021 年 7 月以來的單月新高紀錄。

二、 AI Power 帶動大尺寸 MLCC(Mega Cap 、NP0、高
壓 X7/X8 同步放量 ) 
成長由 Mega Cap 、NP0 及高溫高壓 X7/X8 等大尺寸特殊 MLCC
構成，主要切入 AI Server 的 PSU 、BBU 、CBU 等 Power 端， AI
營收占比由 1Q26 的 6.5%、2Q 的 9.2%，預估 3Q、4Q 進一步提升
至 12% 、16% ，為產品組合及毛利率改善核心。  
NP0 主要用於 LLC 迴路，目前約占公司營收 10% 且已滿載； Mega 
Cap 則於 2H26 開始大量放量 ， 產品以既有MLCC 進行後加工及堆
疊，一般產品 ASP 若為 1，Mega Cap 約可達 7~8 倍，隨堆疊數增
加單價可再提高，因此不僅增加出貨量，亦可明顯提高既有產能
產值。 X7/X8 則受惠 AI Power 高溫、高壓及高可靠度要求同步增
加，公司大尺寸產品主要供應兩大台系電源廠。供給端目前已轉
為產能受限，公司 2Q 後產線接近滿載、訂單能見度約 12 個月，
2026 年楊梅一廠整體 MLCC 產能較去年增加約 30% ；另一方面，
2H26 既有產品平均調漲約 30% ，價格效益逐步反映，新 AI 產品
則以高 ASP 產品放量為主。展望 2027 年，楊梅二廠預計 2Q27 完
工、2H27 量產，整體 MLCC 產能較現有水位再增加接近一倍，其
中 NP0 產能較既有增加約 60 ～ 70% ；六甲粉末廠產能增加約
50% ，並由 250nm 進一步往 200nm 高階粉材發展，配合中高壓及
大尺寸高階 MLCC 擴產，成長強勁。

三、預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 8.31/15.15 元 
2H26 受惠一廠新增產能、既有產品漲價及 Mega Cap 開始放量，

預估 3Q26 ／4Q26 營收 16.86 ／20.93 億元， AI 營收占比由 2Q 的
9.2% 提高至 12% ／16%；隨 NP0、Mega Cap 及 X7/X8 等高毛利特
殊品占比提升，預估毛利率由 2Q26 的 28.8% 進一步升至 3Q／4Q
的 31.58% ／34.07%。預 估2026 年營收 61.72 億元（YoY +52.4% ）、
毛利率 31.28% 、營業利益率約 24.2% ，稅後 EPS 8.31 元。

2027 年除 Mega Cap 及 NP0 完整貢獻全年外， 2H27 楊梅二廠新增
接近一倍 MLCC 產能開始加入，其中 NP0 較既有產能增加 60～
70% ，六甲粉末新廠亦同步開出，支撐高階產品持續放量。預估
AI 營收占比全年提升至 22% ，全年營收 103.56 億元（ YoY 
+67.8% ）；高 ASP 特殊品占比進一步提高，惟 2H 新廠折舊開始
反映於製造成本 ， 預估全年毛利率35.17% 、 營業利益率約28.0% ，
稅後 EPS 15.15 元。

四、投資建議：買進，目標價 378 元 
信昌電由過去消費性 、 網通及車用產品 ， 逐步轉向AI Server Power
所需的大尺寸、高壓、高功率特殊 MLCC ，現階段需求已由景氣
復甦轉為 AI 帶動的結構性成長。短期受惠既有產品漲價、一廠擴
產及 Mega Cap 放量 ， 中期則由楊梅二廠與六甲粉末廠開出支撐量
能，且 Mega Cap 、NP0 及 X7/X8 等特殊品 ASP 與毛利率均高於
既有產品，使營收成長同時具備量增及產品組合升級效果，且為
台廠唯二有能力供應全球大尺寸高壓高容 MLCC 廠商，以 27 年
EPS 15.15 元計算，給予 25 倍本益比，目標價 378 元，建議買進。

貳、公 司 概 況 
一、公司簡介  
信昌電成立於 1990 年，主要從事 MLCC 、貼片電阻及介電陶瓷粉
末的研發與製造，是國內是台灣唯一具備介電陶瓷粉末自製能力
的公司。公司台灣主要生產據點包括桃園廠及楊梅廠，桃園廠主
要生產陶瓷粉末及貼片電阻，楊梅廠則以 MLCC 為主；另楊梅二
廠及六甲粉末新廠預計 2027 年下半年量產。信昌電長期聚焦高功
率 、 高電壓 、 大尺寸及高可靠度被動元件 ， 產品包括MLCC、Chip-R
及介電陶瓷粉末。其中 2026 年上半年營收以 MLCC 為主，占約
68% ，其次為電阻約 16% 及粉末約 12% ，主力仍集中於 MLCC 。
公司應用市場則逐步由過去消費性電子轉向工業、 AI Server 
Power 、車用及特殊應用。從終端應用來看，公司工業應用占比持
續提升， 2026 年 7 月已達 53% ，主要受 AI 電源及其他高功率工
業需求帶動； AI 應用營收占比則由 1Q26 的 6.5% 提升至 2Q26 的
9.2% ，公司預期 4Q26 可進一步提升至 16% 。

二、 AI Power 帶動需求， Mega Cap 、NP0 開始放量  
信昌電 AI 產品主要集中在 Power 端，並非主機板上的小尺寸
MLCC ，而是 PSU 、BBU 等電源系統使用的大尺寸、高壓及高可
靠度產品。過去手機、 NB 等消費性產品對大尺寸元件需求低，大
尺寸 MLCC 在整體市場用量占比不高； 2025 年大於 1206 尺寸產
品用量占整體 MLCC 僅約 1.49% ，供應商因此長期較少。隨 AI 伺
服器功率快速提升，電源端電壓、功率及可靠度要求同步提高，
大尺寸 MLCC 需求開始明顯增加，公司長期深耕的產品規格因而
由過去利基市場轉為 AI Power 的重要元件。

AI 產品主要以 Mega Cap 及高壓低損耗 NP0 兩項特殊品為主 。NP0
具低損耗、耐高溫及穩定性，主要使用於 Power 的 LLC 迴路，目
前約占公司營收 10% ；隨功率提升， LLC 需要以多顆 NP0 堆疊達
到所需容量，公司產品規格亦由 630V ～1000V 持續往 1250V 發
展。高階 NP0 單機用量由 A100 世代約 800 ～1,000 顆，H100 增加
至 1,800 ～2,000 顆，至 GB300 已提升至約 3,000 ～5,000 顆，後續
800V HVDC 架構仍將維持大量高壓元件需求。

Mega Cap 則為堆疊式 MLCC ，主要訴求高可靠度、散熱及降低斷
裂風險，可應用於 AI Server Power 、BBU 及 AI Mini Station 。過
去 Mega Cap 因單價高、使用量有限，但 AI 設備整機價值高，電
源端對元件失效容忍度低，可靠度的重要性高於單純元件成本，
因此開始增加使用。 Mega Cap 單價約為一般單體 MLCC 的 7～8

倍以上，依堆疊數量增加後 ASP 還可進一步提高；製程上則是在
既有 MLCC 基礎上進行後加工及堆疊，不需要同比例增加前段產
能，因此特殊品占比提高可明顯增加單位產能產值及毛利。

以 AI Server 次系統來看，大尺寸高壓 MLCC 主要使用於 PSU 、
BBU 、主板及 Switch ，其中 PSU 與 BBU 用量最高；估算 GB300
／ Vera Rubin 等世代單一機櫃電源與備援系統的大尺寸高壓
MLCC 用量可達上萬顆。隨 GB300 及 Vera Rubin 於 2H26 加速拉
貨，公司 AI 營收占比已由 1Q26 的 6.5% 提升至 2Q26 的 9.2%，預
估 Q3、Q4 分別提升至 12% 、16% ；其中 Mega Cap 與客戶已合作
驗證約 2～3 年，真正放量集中於 2H26 ，因此下半年 AI 營收成長
斜率將明顯高於上半年。本報告預估 27 年全年 AI 營收占比約
22% ，4Q27 約 26% 。

三、特殊品供給吃緊，舊品漲價、新品放量推升毛利  
大尺寸 MLCC 過去占整體 MLCC 用量僅約 1.49% ，市場規模相對
小，供應商長期投入有限；大尺寸產品單價遠高於一般 MLCC ，
價值占總體 MLCC 約 20～30%。目前台灣高功率大尺寸 MLCC 主
要供應商為信昌電及 H 公司，公司主要供應台系兩家電源大廠。

日系廠商雖具備相關技術，但產能主要投入小尺寸高容產品，大
尺寸 X7／X8 及 NP0 投入相對有限；陸系廠商目前在高壓 Power
特殊品的供應能力亦較有限，信昌電長期投入大尺寸 MLCC ，且
有地理優勢配合台系客戶，因此本輪新需求主要集中在高容、高
壓特殊品，而非 2018 年一般品 MLCC 全面缺貨的情況，基本面正
向許多。  
需求自 2Q26 開始明顯轉強，公司目前訂單遠超現有產能，訂單能
見度約 12 個月，交期已拉長至約 30 週，NP0 等 AI 特殊品則優先
安排生產。供給吃緊下，公司 2H26 針對既有產品及舊客戶進行價
格調整，平均漲幅約 30% ，部分規格依供需狀況有所差異； AI 等
新產品則是在 Design-in 時即依特殊規格訂價，目前主要成長來自
需求放量而非漲價，後續價格待新年度合約重談時再進行調整。
因此下半年營運有兩項不同動能：既有產品受惠漲價提高 ASP ，
AI 端則由 Mega Cap 、NP0 等原本單價與毛利較高的特殊品快速放
量，推升整體產品組合。

此產品組合變化已反映在獲利能力。 2024 年公司成長仍主要來自
電動車，至 4Q25 開始轉向 AI Server Power ；2Q26 客戶確認需求
後加速拉貨，單季營收 QoQ 成長約 30% ，毛利率提升至 28.8% ，
相較去年同期增加 5.2 個百分點 。 公司表示毛利率改善主要來自產
品組合，而非漲價，因今年出貨已由去年同期車用產品轉向 AI 大
尺寸、高功率及高壓特殊品；隨 2H26 Mega Cap 放量、 AI 占比提
高及既有產品漲價效益逐步反映。

三、訂單遠超於產能， 2H27 新廠產能滿載貢獻獲利  
信昌電目前 MLCC 及材料產能稼動率滿載， AI 及大尺寸產品訂單
已超過現有供應能力，因此短期營收成長主要受產能限制。楊梅
一廠持續進行去瓶頸及產能擴充， 2026 年 MLCC 產能較去年增加
約 30% ；由於 Mega Cap 屬後加工產品，公司亦可透過提高 Mega 
Cap 產品占比，在前段產能有限下提高單位產能產值。

下一階段擴產則集中於楊梅二廠及六甲粉末新廠。楊梅二廠主要
生產高壓、低損耗及高溫穩定型 MLCC ，預計 2Q27 完工、 2H27
開始量產，開出後 NP0 相關產能較目前可再增加約 60～70% ；六
甲新廠則建置新一代自動化陶瓷粉末產線，除提高生產效率及粉
末產能約 50% ，亦將粉末粒徑由既有 250nm 進一步往 200nm 發
展，以支援更高階 MLCC 產品。兩座新廠皆預計 2027H2 量產，
將成為目前產能吃緊後的下一階段成長動能。

除 AI 外，公司亦逐步布局機器人、自駕車及低軌衛星等高功率應
用。機器人主要切入關節馬達、無框力矩馬達及控制板，隨系統

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估 
一、預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 為 8.31 元， YoY+156.5% 
以 2Q26 營運為基礎逐季推估， 7／8 月營收已分別達 5.37 ／5.52
億元 ， 考量楊梅一廠2026 年整體 MLCC 產能較去年增加約 30% 、
2Q 後產能滿載，以及既有產品 2H26 漲價與 Mega Cap 開始放量，
預估 3Q26 ／4Q26 營收分別為 16.86 ／20.93 億元， AI 營收占比由
2Q 的 9.2% 提升至 12% ／16%。預估 2026 年營收 61.72 億元，YoY 
+52.2% ；隨 NP0、Mega Cap 及 X7/X8 等高 ASP 特殊品占比提升，
毛利率由 2025 年的 24.3% 升至 31.3% ，營業利益率由 16.8% 提升
至 24.2%。 全年營業利益14.92 億元 ，YoY +119.1%； 稅後淨利13.37
億元， YoY +98.9% ；受 10 月現金減資後股本降至 16.09 億元，稅
後 EPS 為 8.31 元， YoY +156.5% 。營業利益增幅顯著高於營收，
反映 AI 高毛利特殊品放量、既有產品漲價及產能滿載帶動獲利結
構明顯改善。

二、預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 為 15.15 元， YoY +82.3%  
展望 2027 年， AI Server Power 需求持續增長， Mega Cap 由 2H26
放量轉為全年貢獻， NP0、X7/X8 等高壓高功率 MLCC 同步成長，
預估 AI 營收占比由 4Q26 的 16% 進一步提升至全年 20% 以上。產
能方面， 2026 年一廠新增 30% 產能完整貢獻全年， 2H27 楊梅二廠
量產後整體 MLCC 產能較現有水位再增加接近一倍，其中 NP0 產
能較既有增加 60～70% ；六甲粉末廠產能亦增加 50% ，支撐高階
大尺寸 MLCC 擴產 。 預估2027 年營收 103.56 億元 ，YoY +67.8% ；
隨高 ASP 特殊品比重持續提高 ， 毛利率升至35.2%、YoY +3.9ppts ，
營業利益率提升至 28.0% 、YoY +3.8ppts 。全年營業利益 28.97 億

## Document 292: The Renminbi Appears to be Undervalued by 30 Percent (2026-09-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Renminbi Appears to be Undervalued by 30 Percent (2026-09-01).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 14
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
The Renminbi Appears to be Undervalued by 30
Percent
China’s official current account data understate the scale of its external surplus
and, by extension, the degree of renminbi undervaluation.
PUBLISHED
September 1, 2026 3:07 p.m.
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
Harvard’s Dr. Gopinath (formerly of the IMF) asked
about the basis for my estimate (in Greg Ip’s excellent
WSJ column) that the RMB is now undervalued by
30 percent.*
The simple answer: I used the IMF’s own current
account methodology but didn’t take China’s reported
current account surplus at face value. I believe that
the surplus is significantly understated, as it doesn’t
map to other known numbers about China’s economy

and trade performance.**
The IMF’s current account gap methodology hinges
on the difference between the observed current
account and the “current account” norm calculated by

### Page 3

3
the IMF (on the basis of demographics, levels of
development, the net international investment
position, and other variables). For China, that current
account norm is now estimated at 0.6 percent of
GDP (2026 ESR, Table 2.6)
A cyclical adjustment added 0.2 percent of GDP to
the estimated norm for 2025. The end-2025 surplus
was 3.8 percent of GDP. The 3 percent of GDP gap
resulted in an estimated undervaluation of 21 percent
as the IMF’s work implies that a 7 percent move in

the exchange rate (or equivalent changes in other
policies) reduces the surplus by a percentage point of
GDP.
A bigger surplus thus implies a bigger “gap” and a
bigger estimated undervaluation (for a given
constellation of policies).
And I think the 3.8 percent of GDP surplus for end-
2025, now up to 4 percent of GDP in the last four
quarters of data, is understated. I am not the only one
who thinks by the way; the raw customs data—
adjusted for the observed deficit in travel services and

the recent surge in gold imports—implies an overall
surplus of close to 6 percent of GDP these days.
There are three reasons to believe that the reported
current account surplus understates the scale of
China’s real surplus:

### Page 4

4
1. The reported investment income deficit makes no
economic sense.
The reported deficit on investment income is very
hard to understand. You don’t have to take my word
on this. The IMF looked into China’s investment
income balance and agreed (2025 ESR, Box 1.1). So
did Ma and Wei.
The fact that China reports an investment income
deficit of over $100 billion with net foreign assets of
over $4 trillion isn’t easily explained. Yes, there can be
a difference in return on foreign direct investment in

China and Chinese direct investment abroad. But
with roughly equal levels of investment in both
directions, that gap in returns on direct investment
would need to be between 6 and 8 percentage points
to offset the $120-160 billion in interest income that
would be expected on China’s net holdings of foreign
bonds and other interest-paying assets at a

### Page 5

5
conservative 3 to 4 percent interest rate (CDB and
state commercial bank loans, if anything, should carry
a higher rate, as the CDB lends at a big markup over
the U.S. policy rate).
And there is no real evidence that foreign direct
investors are earning a ton of money inside China
these days. The auto companies certainly are not.
Many have abandoned their Chinese JVs, or are using
them primarily to supply global markets. The
exception—Apple—actually proves the rule. Apple

does have a nice business in China. But China’s 2022
statistical adjustments should move Apple’s returns
out of the “direct investment” line as Apple’s IP
returns accrue to its Irish subsidiary, not its Chinese
subsidiary, and into the balance of payments as a small
goods deficit.***
The evolution of the investment income line item
also doesn’t make any sense. The investment income
deficit increased a lot in 2019 and 2020. Certainly,
interest rates were low globally in 2020. But that

doesn’t explain why the investment income balance
remained in a large deficit even after global interest
rates soared. The returns on China’s large and growing
external loan and bond portfolio should have soared,
but they didn’t. That makes no sense.
China in Q1 reported something like a 2 percent
return on its foreign bonds and overseas lending. That
doesn’t compute, as SAFE eked out a 3 percent
return in the low-for-long era after the GFC and can

### Page 6

6
get more than that from a portfolio of five-year
Treasury notes and five-year bunds.****
2. China started importing a ton of gold in 2026.
China’s gold imports have gone from around 0.5
percent of GDP to 1.5 percent of GDP in a couple of
quarters. That is strange—especially as China’s rising
interest in gold came as gold prices fell.
Gold is a strange import. If the gold isn’t going
directly to the central bank, it counts as an import
even though it is often held as a financial asset. And if

the gold is going directly to the central bank, it is
counted as the purchase of a financial asset. Some
even think SAFE is buying gold through domestic
brokers as a means of shrinking the reported surplus.
It thus isn’t really controversial to move the gold
import line out of the current account and over to the
financial account.
3. China started fudging its balance of payments
goods number when it changed its balance of
payments methodology back in 2022.

That change is now statistically faded into the 2021
data as well.

### Page 7

7
The changes were in theory an attempt to implement
the IMF’s latest guidance (BPM6, which the U.S.
hasn’t done). While the IMF has recognized
problems with China’s reported investment income
deficit, the IMF statistics department hasn’t given an
inch on the new goods data, and argues that China,
unlike the U.S., has at least tried to follow its
statistical guidance.
But China’s actual implementation of the IMF’s
guidance was suspect to say the least. I covered this

debate in detail a year ago (Apple plays a large role in
China’s argument). And the IMF refuses to assess
whether the results of the new methodology are really
an improvement over the results of the old
methodology.
One of the adjustments that SAFE outlined to the
IMF back in 2024—the “in China for China”
adjustment for firms that don’t have an onshore JV
and rely on contract manufacturing—is in principle

### Page 8

8
reasonable. Moving Apple’s profit out of income over
to the BOP goods line would be consistent with
Apple’s treatment in the Irish balance of payments
data, but it should have produced a $20-$30 billion
change in the goods number, not a $300 billion
change. And moving Apple’s “in China” profit from
one line of the current account to another shouldn’t
have changed the overall current account numbers.
The other adjustment, which arbitrarily reduces

exports in the balance of payments down relative to
customs on a never demonstrated claim that contract
manufacturers were inflating their exports in the
customs data by using a firm’s global wholesale price
in customs rather than the amount actually received,
simply doesn’t check out. The IMF, to the best of my
knowledge, has never looked into the details here
either.
It is relatively easy to construct a consistent time
series using China’s pre-2021/22 methodology for

calculating its balance of payments goods data (the
standard adjustment moves insurance and freight
charges over to services). The new methodology, at
least until recently, really did lower the goods surplus,
and thus the overall current account surplus.
There was clearly a massive downward adjustment to
balance of payments goods for a while, without
offsetting changes in other lines in the current
account.

### Page 9

9
I was ginned up about this for a while. It really had a
big impact on the 2023 and 2024 current account
surplus numbers and thus changed the IMF’s overall
assessment of China. But—for complex reasons—the
impact of this adjustment has shrunk, and it is now
less significant than the investment income and gold
distortions.***** See the grey and gold bars in the
chart below.
When these factors are taken into account, I estimate
that China’s surplus should be around 5.5 percent of

GDP—which, without the cyclical adjustment, works
out to a 35 percent undervaluation (33 percent, with
the cyclical adjustment).

### Page 10

10
I also used the IMF’s methodology to estimate what
the undervaluation should have been in the past
(without the cyclical adjustment, as I cannot estimate
that reliably).
The larger estimated undervaluation checks out
against a range of other indicators.
The Big Mac Index (37 percent undervaluation).
Industry-specific cost comparisons. Greg Ip has a
nice chart from Goldman. European auto analysts
think China has a 30 percent edge in production
costs.

It is also clear in China’s export outperformance
relative to global trade. And the breadth of this
outperformance.
At the end of the day though, it doesn’t currently
matter too much whether the renminbi is

### Page 11

11
undervalued by 20 percent or 35 percent. There is
consensus that there is a significant undervaluation.
Fixing that is, by far, the easiest way to address
concerns about China’s ever-rising trade surplus—and
to get Xi to reconsider China’s growth model and do
more to support internal demand.
* Dr. Gopinath observes that the IMF’s current
account gap can be closed through a combination of
exchange rate moves, domestic policy changes and
changes in the international environment (i.e. slower

demand growth abroad, slower growth in global
trade), not just through exchange rate changes. That is
a fair point. Though I would note that there isn’t
much evidence over the last 15 years that Chinese
demand growth spills over into import demand.
Think of an industrial policy for everything. Or, more
formally, income elasticity that is way below one.

### Page 12

12
That implies the exchange rate, which does seem to
impact export performance more than the IMF
believed in the past, will need to do much of the work.
** The customs surplus can (and has been) checked
against counterparty data. The travel deficit now
broadly maps to observed tourism flows. And the
reported NIIP is smaller than it should be given
China’s accumulated surpluses. The gross asset and
liability position in the IIP data can nonetheless be

used to generate an independent estimate of
investment income (part of the current account).
*** I agree, for what it is worth. My concern is that
China never showed its work, and there isn’t any
evidence that moving Apple’s “in China” return over
to the BOP goods line items was matched by
offsetting adjustments to either the services line (if
the “in China” profit was previously counted as a
royalty to Apple Ireland) or the income line (if it was

previously added to the income line, adding to
China’s deficit in FDI income). The current account
impact of moving around Apple’s $20 to 30 billion
“in China” profit should be zero.
**** The duration on China’s visible holdings of
Treasuries is a bit above the norm for reserve
managers, and China holds a ton of U.S. Agency
bonds (MBS), so it should get an above average
return on its dollar reserves. SAFE isn’t particularly
risk averse.

### Page 13

13
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.

NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International (CC BY-NC-
ND 4.0) License.

CHINA ARMS INDUSTRIES AND TRADE
***** I would like to think my efforts here had an
impact on SAFE, if by nothing else making folks
aware that there was an issue. But in reality, SAFE
appears to be adjusting the BOP goods line to limit
reported errors, and as the renminbi stabilized,
settlement conversion increased, and the state banks
started adding more to their financial assets, SAFE
needed to report a higher current account surplus to
map to the larger observed buildup of Chinese assets

abroad.

### Page 14

14

## Document 295: The Wrap on Asia_ Asia's Currency Puzzle (2026-09-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Wrap on Asia_ Asia's Currency Puzzle (2026-09-17).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
1
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WRAP ON ASIA
Mapping out Asia ...
Illiana Jain
Economist, Westpac Group
P: +61 403  908 032
E: illiana.jain@westpac.com.au
• Asian growth forecasts have generally been revised
higher over the past year, supported by resilient domestic
demand and export growth, with the tech sector a dominant

contributor to production and investment.
• Exchange rates have largely failed to reflect these economic
fundamentals. The INR has depreciated 7.1% against the
USD over the past year, while the JPY has fallen 4.1%. KRW
is up 3.7%, but these gains are nascent.
• Regional FX performance is also weak in real effective terms
despite the strength in trade and the potential for regional
economic development.
• Increasingly, capital allocation decisions are offsetting the

support provided by economic fundamentals.
• India’s response has been to actively attract foreign currency
savings back into the domestic financial system. In June, the
RBI introduced a concessional FX swap facility and removed
interest rate ceilings on FCNR(B) deposits.
• By lowering hedging costs, banks were able to offer deposit
rates approaching 7%, creating a powerful incentive for
funds held overseas to be repatriated to India.
• The scheme was successful, with deposits reaching

US$127.2bn and the programme closing a month earlier
than planned due to strong demand.
• The inflows have helped support the INR while reducing
pressure on the RBI to intervene.
• However, the FCNR(B) deposits mature within three to five
years, creating a risk of future capital outflows.
• India faced a similar challenge when around US$34bn of
funds raised during the 2013 taper tantrum matured in 2016.
• Sizeable outflows occurred, but they did not trigger a

disorderly adjustment in INR as the RBI accumulated
reserves and prepared for the maturities in advance.
• The more important development is the changing nature
of India’s external financing. Remittance flows are
increasingly sourced from higher-income migrants in
advanced economies, and these funds are being deployed
as investment capital rather than household savings and
spending.
• As financial markets deepen, future maturities are
increasingly likely to be invested into other domestic assets

rather than repatriated offshore.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WRAP ON ASIA
• South Korea’s experience highlights how strong economic
performance no longer guarantees currency appreciation.
• Export growth remains robust, profitability among major
technology firms is near record levels, and AI-related
investment continues to support activity.

• Despite this, the KRW has remained weak as Korean
households and institutions increasingly allocate capital
offshore rather than holding returns domestically.
• Financial account dynamics are therefore becoming an
increasingly important determinant of exchange rate
outcomes.
• South Korea recorded a US$46.7bn financial account
surplus over the past year, including a US$29.9bn surplus in
portfolio investment.
• In short, rather than supporting domestic financial markets,

a growing share of Korea’s excess savings is being invested
in international assets.
• Of particular note, Korean holdings of US securities have
climbed to almost US$1tn.
• The scale and pace of these flows help explain why the
KRW has struggled to appreciate on enduring strength
in the trade position. Korea’s challenge is not generating
savings, but retaining them.
• Japan’s headline current account surplus of JPY8.7trn in
Q2 2026 is just JPY5.4trn on a cashflow basis, reflecting

the growing share of overseas earnings being accumulated
offshore.
• At the same time, wide interest-rate differentials have
continued to encourage households and institutional
investors to allocate savings offshore, reinforcing yen
weakness.
• Rising JGB yields and higher FX hedging costs are beginning
to reverse these incentives, making domestic assets more
attractive relative to the foreign alternatives.
• Still, the gap is wide and inertia is strong. A material

appreciation in the yen against the US dollar will take time
and the right circumstances.

## Document 300: UBS APAC Focus China Internet Sector How China's AI mode

Source PDF: all report pdf/UBS APAC Focus China Internet Sector How China's AI mode.pdf

### Page 1

ab 16 June 2026
Global Research
China Internet Sector
APAC Focus:  How China's AI models compete
on cost efficiency
In the global AI race, model intelligence is key to assessing competitiveness and
commercialisation. However, as AI adoption shifts  to scaled deployment, we expect
cost efficiency to become increasingly important. Through targeted R&D,
architectural innovations, engineering optimisation and open-source collaboration,
we believe China's companies are rapidly closing the performance gap with global

leaders at markedly lower costs, making them well positioned to gain share in the
sizeable, expanding global AI TAM. We highlight  MiniMax, Zhipu, Alibaba,
Tencent and Baidu under our coverage as major companies in this theme,
together with private companies DeepSeek, Kimi, ByteDance and StepFun.
Assessing the cost leadership of China's AI models
We estimate the training costs for China's models (using MiniMax and Zhipu as
examples)  are less than 10% of those of global leaders such as OpenAI and Anthropic,

while the average API price of major China models is below 20% of comparable global
peers. However, China's frontier models are closing the capability gap with rapid
iteration and improving intelligence, gaining global traction in token usage while
preserving healthy gross margins.
Structural and sustainable efficiency gains in model training and inference
Supported by our technical analysis, we believe this reflects Chinese models' structural
advantages in model training and inferences. In training, China's model providers use

multiple approaches to reduce workload (via smaller parameters and sparse attention)
and improve GPU throughput, helped by targeted R&D and open-source ecosystems. In
inference, key innovations span model architecture (e.g., MoE, attention mechanism,
KV cache and lower precision), engineering optimisation (e.g.,  P/D separation, batching
and scheduling) and infrastructure (incl. domestic chip adaptation).
Reshaping global AI competition through superior cost-performance

We expect the global AI market to burgeon, with long-term TAM potentially surpassing
US$10trn, and see considerable scope for China's models to gain global market share.
As AI adoption moves from experimentation to scaled deployment,  companies are
shifting from maximising AI token usage (i.e., “token maxxing”) towards more ROI-
driven resource allocation. In our view, this shift should improve market recognition of
Chinese models' cost-performance advantages. We expect the global model market to

Q: Can China's AI models continue to close the performance gap at lower R&D costs?
Likely. We observe a narrowing gap in model intelligence between China's providers and global
leaders, yet R&D spending at China's AI labs remains considerably lower than those of US peers.

While distillation improves efficiency in model development, we believe China's model providers
adopt multiple approaches to reduce workloads (such as smaller parameters and sparse attention)
and improve GPU throughput, supported by targeted R&D and open-source ecosystems.
Q: Can China's models keep prices competitive for API services while  maintaining
reasonable gross margins?
Likely. We believe Chinese AI models' competitive API prices are not driven by high subsidies or a loss-

leading strategy, but by structural advantages in inference efficiency. We note key technological
innovations across: 1) model architecture such as mixture of experts (MoE), attention mechanisms,
KV cache and lower precision; 2) engineering optimisation, including P/D separation, batching and
scheduling; and 3) infrastructure, such as domestic chip adaptation. Despite prices at under 20% of
those of comparable US peers, API gross margins for China's AI models are around 20-40%, with

upside potential  if prices improve with model performance or additional gains in inference efficiency.
Q: How will China's AI models reshape the global model competitive landscape?
We view competition as a secondary concern in light of the rapidly expanding global AI TAM, which
we estimate could surpass US$10trn. As model supply is concentrated in the US and China, we
believe China's model providers should have meaningful opportunities for global expansion. As AI

adoption scales, we also expect demand to become increasingly tiered. While frontier models may
retain pricing premiums for complex, high-value tasks, cost-performance models could see broader
adoption in high-volume, ROI-sensitive workloads, creating global share-gain opportunities for
China's AI models in the longer term.
As AI adoption moves from experimentation to scaled deployment, we believe cost efficiency is likely
to become a key competitive factor alongside model intelligence. Based on our detailed technical

analysis, we believe China's AI model providers demonstrate structural and sustainable advantages in
training and inference efficiency, which make them well positioned to gain share in the large,
expanding global AI TAM, in our view. We highlight MiniMax, Zhipu, Alibaba and Tencent under our
coverage as major companies in this theme, together with private companies DeepSeek, Kimi,
ByteDance and StepFun.
1) China's frontier models have reached around 90% of the intelligence level of leading US models,

improving from around 60% in 2023, according to Artificial Analysis. 2) Total R&D costs of China's
listed AI model developers Zhipu/MiniMax amounted to US$0.5bn/US$0.3bn in 2025. This is around
one-tenth of Anthropic's R&D spending, and even lower relative to  OpenAI. 3) API pricing for China's
model  is significantly lower than those of US peers. Overall, we estimate API prices for China's
frontier models averages below 20% of comparable  US peers with similar  intelligence. 4) MiniMax's

M2.7 has gross margins of over 40%, broadly in line with Anthropic (roughly 40% in 2025, mainly
API and B2B-driven), and also comparable to  OpenAI's 40%/33% gross margin in 2024/25, which
benefits from a larger revenue contribution from consumer-facing, subscription-based services. Our
channel checks indicate API gross margins for China's major models are roughly in the 20-40%
range.
We believe investors do not fully appreciate or have strong conviction in Chinese AI labs' cost

leadership, and instead expect materially higher R&D investments and widening losses to support
model training and rising token demand. Investors seem also concerned that model offerings will
become commoditised over time, leading to intense price competition and potentially unsustainable
business models for China's AI model providers.

### Page 4

China Internet Sector 16 June 2026 ab 4
Figure 2: Glossary of key AI model concepts
Category Concept Explanation
Token The basic unit of text processed by a model. One token may correspond to a Chinese character or part of an
English word.
Parameters The internal weights of a model. More parameters usually indicate larger model capacity, but also higher
training and inference cost.
Context window The maximum amount of text / tokens a model can process in one request. A longer context window

supports longer documents and agentic tasks.
Long-horizon task A complex task that requires many steps, tool calls or extended context, such as AI coding, debugging or
project planning.
AI agent An AI system that can plan, use tools and take actions to complete tasks, rather than only responding in a
chatbot format.
Tool use / function calling The ability of a model to call external tools, APIs, databases or software functions to retrieve information or

execute actions.
MoE (Mixture-of-Experts) A model architecture where only a subset of parameters is activated for each token, reducing compute
requirements.
Sparse attention A design that allows the model to focus on the most relevant parts of the context rather than every token.
Training / inference precision The numerical format used in model computation, such as FP16, BF16 and FP8. Lower precision can reduce
compute and memory cost while maintaining model quality.

Quantization Reducing the numerical precision of model weights or computation to lower memory usage and inference
cost, often with limited impact on model quality.
Prefill The stage where the model reads and processes the input prompt before generating any output.
Decode The stage where the model generates output tokens one by one.
KV cache A stored representation of previously processed tokens that avoids recomputing the full context during
inference.

Prompt caching / cached input Previously processed input tokens that can be reused and charged at a lower cached-input price, instead of
being recomputed and charged as full input.
MFU (Model FLOPs Utilisation) A measure of how effectively a GPU’s theoretical compute capacity is utilised during model training or
inference.
Batching Grouping multiple requests together so GPUs can process them more efficiently.
Scheduling The system-level process of assigning requests to GPUs and managing task execution to improve utilisation

and latency.
Prefill-Decode separation Separating prefill and decode workloads onto different GPU resources to improve utilisation and reduce
latency.
TPS (tokens per second) The speed at which a model generates output tokens. Higher TPS means faster response generation.
TTFT (time to first token) The time between submitting a request and receiving the first output token. Lower TTFT improves perceived
latency.
GPU-hour One GPU running for one hour. It is a common unit for measuring training or inference compute cost.

Model router A system that selects the most suitable model for each task based on quality, cost, latency, reliability or
other routing rules.
AI gateway A unified access layer for multiple models / providers, often handling model routing, API management,
monitoring, security and cost control.
API pricing The price charged for model usage, usually based on tokens. Pricing is commonly split into uncached input
tokens, cached input tokens and output tokens.

Token plan A prepaid or subscription-style model usage package, often with a fixed monthly fee and token quota /
allowance.
MaaS (Model-as-a-Service) A business model where customers access models through APIs or cloud platforms and pay based on
usage.

Closing the model performance gap at lower R&D
costs
We see a narrowing performance gap between AI models from the US and
China. While US remains at the forefront globally in model intelligence, China's model
providers are iterating rapidly and are catching up on key capabilities in:
 Overall intelligence of text models : China's frontier models have reached
around 90% of the intelligence level of top US models, improving from about

60% in 2023, according to data from Artificial Analysis.
 AI coding: China's leading models such as Qwen3.7-Max and DeepSeek V4-Pro
are comparable with previous generations of overseas models  (for example,
Claude Opus 4.6), although China's models still lag the latest frontier models such
as Claude Fable 5  and GPT-5.5.
 Multimodality: We find it encouraging that China's AI models have
demonstrated their global competitiveness. For instance, four of the top five

video-generation models globally are from China's developers, according to
Artificial Analysis.

China's AI labs are narrowing the performance gap at notably lower R&D
spending than US peers. Total R&D costs of listed Chinese AI model developers Zhipu/
MiniMax were US$0.5bn/US$0.3bn in 2025. This represents only around one-tenth of
Anthropic's R&D spending, and even less relative to  OpenAI, even when considering
only  server costs (that is computing costs) for the two US leaders. In our view, this
suggests China’s  progress reflects a catch-up in  capability as well as significantly more

efficient R&D spending.

computing, staff and other costs.  OpenAI's and Anthropic's R&D costs include only computing costs.
Distillation helps, but does not fully explain Chinese companies' efficient model

development.  We view model distillation as a teacher-student process, in which a
'student' model (often with fewer parameters) learns to mimic the outputs or behaviour
of a stronger 'teacher' model. Based on our channel checks with industry experts,
distillation can improve model capability with shorter training time and lower compute
costs, but its effectiveness is more visible in tasks with verifiable outcomes, repeatable
processes or easily imitable patterns. In contrast, distillation is less effective for

capabilities that are less verifiable, rely on complex multi-step processes or depend on
underlying architecture and system-level engineering ( Figure 7D is t illa t io n  h e lp s  in  v e r if ia b le  t a s k s  b u t  g e n e r a lly  h a s  lim it a t io n s  in  f r o n t ie r  c a p a b ilit ie s ). We believe  China's
progress in frontier models suggests capability gains beyond distillation alone. For
instance, Zhipu has demonstrated strong long-horizon reasoning, while MiniMax

models feature advanced multimodal capabilities.

Demystifying  China AI labs' R&D efficiency
China's model labs follow a more focused R&D strategy
In terms of R&D spending allocation, we observe differing strategic focus
between the US and China.  For US frontier labs, we see a larger share of R&D
spending being used for experimentation and path exploration , reflecting a stronger
emphasis on pushing the upper limits of model intelligence, supported by greater

compute resources. These experiments may span pre-training (non-Transformer
architecture for example), data (such as data curation and synthetic-data recipes), and
post-training stages (including  reinforcement learning from human feedback [RLHF] and
reinforcement learning-based reasoning and alignment). Taking OpenAI as an example,
Epoch AI data suggests that around 90% of the R&D compute budget is spent on
experimentation, with only about 10% used for final model training.

equivalent numbers of Nvidia H100s, based on dense 8-bit operations per second.
In contrast, we believe China's model labs tend to follow a more focused R&D path ,
which could create meaningful cost-saving potential by reducing the trial-and-error
process. We think China’s different strategic choices versus the US are mainly driven by
two factors.
This document is being provided for the exclusive use of RACHEL CHEN at MOUNTAIN HILL LTD.

### Page 8

China Internet Sector 16 June 2026 ab 8
1. More constrained compute resources. China's labs appear to allocate a smaller
proportion of resources to broad experimentation, focusing instead on formal model
training, targeted capability improvements, and architecture and engineering
optimisation.
2. Open-source ecosystem helps spread experimentation costs across China's AI
labs. While individual labs in China tend to follow a more focused R&D path, the

broader open-source ecosystem allows technical learnings to diffuse across companies.
This means not every lab needs to independently repeat the same broad set of
experiments as closed-source US peers. Instead, once one lab validates an architecture,
training recipe or optimisation method, others can learn from it and build on top of it
(see examples below). In our view, this creates collective R&D leverage and lowers the
marginal cost of capability improvement for China's labs.

 Kimi K2 and GLM-5 have adopted MLA-like latent attention design following
DeepSeek’s earlier validation;
 Zhipu has adopted elements inspired by DeepSeek’s sparse attention mechanism;
 DeepSeek V4 has incorporated techniques related to Moonshot AI and Kimi’s
Muon optimiser;
 Qwen3 and Zhipu GLM-4.5 have implemented GRPO-style RL recipes following
DeepSeek.
